Proofs for Section
6

F.1
Technical lemmas for Sections
6.1
-
6.2

Lemma 3

(Bernstein inequality with growing envelope)
.

Let
X_{1},\dots,X_{n}
be i.i.d. random variables and let
\mathcal{E}_{n}
be an independent dataset (or
\sigma
-field). Let
f_{n}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function measurable with respect to
\mathcal{E}_{n}
, satisfying
\mathbb{E}[f_{n}(X_{i})\mid\mathcal{E}_{n}]=Pf_{n}
,
\operatorname{Var}(f_{n}(X_{i})\mid\mathcal{E}_{n})\leq\sigma_{n}^{2}
, and
|f_{n}(X_{i})|\leq B_{n}
almost surely, for some possibly growing deterministic bound
B_{n}
. Define the empirical mean
P_{n}f_{n}:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f_{n}(X_{i})
. Then for any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(P_{n}f_{n}-Pf_{n}\geq t\mid\mathcal{E}_{n}\right)\leq\exp\left(-\frac{nt^{2}}{2\sigma_{n}^{2}+\frac{2}{3}B_{n}t}\right).

In particular,
P_{n}f_{n}-Pf_{n}=\mathcal{O}_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{n}^{2}}{n}}+\frac{B_{n}}{n}\right).

Lemma 4
.

Assume
D2
and
D3
. Fix
v\in\{0,1\}
and define
\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k):=\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{j\in[n_{k}]:V_{kj}=v}\widehat{\beta}_{K}(X_{kj})
. Suppose that, conditional on the dataset
\mathcal{E}_{K}
, the residuals
R_{k,i}:=\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)
satisfy
\mathbb{E}[R_{k,i}\mid\mathcal{E}_{K}]=0

and assume
\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}=O_{p}(\sigma_{\widehat{\beta}_{K}}^{2})
,
\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{\infty}=O_{p}(B_{\widehat{\beta}_{K}})
. Let
n=\min_{k\in[K]n_{k}}
. Then,

\sup_{k\in[K]}\left|\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\right|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{\widehat{\beta}_{K}}^{2}\log K}{n}}+\frac{B_{\widehat{\beta}_{K}}\log K}{n}\right).

Proof.

Fix
k\in[K]
and apply Lemma
3
conditional on
\mathcal{E}_{K}
to
f_{n,k}(O_{i}):=R_{k,i}
. For any
t>0
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}R_{k,i}\right|\geq t\mid\mathcal{E}_{K}\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{nt^{2}}{2\sigma_{\widehat{\beta}_{K}}^{2}+\frac{2}{3}B_{\widehat{\beta}_{K}}t}\right).

A union bound over
k\in[K]
gives

\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{k\in[K]}\left|\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\right|\geq t\mid\mathcal{E}_{K}\right)\leq 2K\exp\left(-\frac{nt^{2}}{2\sigma_{\widehat{\beta}_{K}}^{2}+\frac{2}{3}B_{\widehat{\beta}_{K}}t}\right).

Setting
t\asymp\sqrt{\sigma_{\widehat{\beta}_{K}}^{2}\log K/n}+B_{\widehat{\beta}_{K}}\log K/n
yields the result.
∎

Lemma 5

(Asymptotic equivalence of debiasing term)
.

Assume
D2
and
D3
. Let
n=\min_{k\in[K]n_{k}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1-v}\left\{\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{j\in[n_{k}]:V_{kj}=v}\widehat{\beta}_{K}(X_{kj})\right\}\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)+O_{p}(N^{-1/2})O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}^{2}\log K}{n}}+\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{\infty}\log K}{n}\right).

Proof of Lemma
5
.

Fix
v\in\{0,1\}
and denote
\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k):=\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{j\in[n_{k}]:V_{kj}=v}\widehat{\beta}_{K}(X_{kj})
. Then, adding and subtracting, we have

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1-v}

\displaystyle\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1-v}\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1-v}\left\{\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\right\}\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr).

We consider the second term on the right-hand side. Let
f_{\widehat{\beta}_{K},\widehat{h}_{K}}:=\left\{\widehat{q}_{K,v}-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})\right\}\left(\mathrm{id}_{Y}-\widehat{h}_{K}\right)
. Then,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1-v}f_{\widehat{\beta}_{K},\widehat{h}_{K}}(O_{ki})=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1-v}\left\{\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\right\}\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr).

By Condition
D3
, the function
f_{\widehat{\beta}_{K},\widehat{h}_{K}}
is uniformly bounded by
O(\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{\infty})
almost surely, since
\widehat{\beta}_{K}
is uniformly bounded by
\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{\infty}
, and both
\widehat{q}_{K,v}
and
\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})
, being averages involving
\widehat{\beta}_{K}
, are also uniformly bounded by
\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{\infty}
. In addition, conditional on the external training data for
\widehat{\beta}_{K}
and
\widehat{h}_{K}
, denoted by
\mathcal{E}_{K}
, the random variable
\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)
has mean zero. Consequently,
f_{\widehat{\beta}_{K},\widehat{h}_{K}}(O_{ki})
is a mean zero random variable conditional on
\mathcal{E}_{K}
, since by independence of the data folds,

E_{K}[f_{\widehat{\beta}_{K},\widehat{h}_{K}}(O_{ki})\mid\mathcal{E}_{K}]=E_{K}\left[\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{j\in[n_{k}]:V_{kj}=v}\widehat{\beta}_{K}(X_{kj})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\mid\mathcal{E}_{K}\right]\cdot E_{K}[Y_{k}-\widehat{h}_{K}(X_{k})\mid\mathcal{E}_{K}]=0.

Moreover, applying Lemma
4
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|f_{\widehat{\beta}_{K},\widehat{h}_{K}}\right\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{k\in[K]}\left|\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\right|O_{p}(1)=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}^{2}\log K}{n}}+\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{\infty}\log K}{n}\right).

It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1-v}\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1-v}\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)+O_{p}(N^{-1/2})O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}^{2}\log K}{n}}+\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{\infty}\log K}{n}\right).

Summing over
v
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1-v}\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)+O_{p}(N^{-1/2})O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}^{2}\log K}{n}}+\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{\infty}\log K}{n}\right).

∎

In the following, denote
\widehat{D}_{K}:=\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(\text{id}_{Y}-\widehat{h}_{K})
and
D_{K}:=\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta_{K})(\text{id}_{Y}-h_{K})
.

Lemma 6

(Asymptotically linear expansion of debiasing term)
.

Assume
D2
,
D3
, and
D5
. Suppose
\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta_{K})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}=o_{p}(1)
,
\|\widehat{h}_{K}-h_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}=o_{p}(1)
. Then,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\widehat{D}_{K}(O_{ki})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}(O_{ki})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\int\widehat{D}_{K}(o_{k})P^{(K)}(do_{k})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

Proof of Lemma
6
.

By
D2
, the function
\widehat{D}_{K}
is fixed conditional on an external independent dataset
\mathcal{E}_{K}
. Moreover, by
D5
(i) and the triangle inequality,
\|\widehat{D}_{K}-D_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}\lesssim\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K}-\beta_{K})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\|\widehat{h}_{K}-h_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}=o_{p}(1)
. Hence, applying Chebyschev’s inequality conditional on
\mathcal{E}_{K}
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\{\widehat{D}_{K}(O_{ki})-P^{(K)}\widehat{D}_{K}\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\{D_{K}(O_{ki})-P^{(K)}D_{K}\}+O_{p}\left(\frac{\|\widehat{D}_{K}-D_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}}{\sqrt{N}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}(O_{ki})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

since
P^{(K)}D_{K}=0
. Rearranging terms, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\widehat{D}_{K}(O_{ki})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}(O_{ki})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\int\widehat{D}_{K}(o_{k})P^{(K)}(do_{k})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

∎

F.2
Proofs of main results

Proof of Theorem
6
.

By
D3
,
D2
, and Lemma
5
,

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1-v}

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{j\in[n_{k}]:V_{kj}=v}\widehat{\beta}_{K}(X_{kj})\right\}\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+O_{p}(N^{-1/2})O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}^{2}\log K}{n}}+\frac{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}\|_{\infty}\log K}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)+o_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{K}^{2}}{N}}\right),

where the final equality follows from the boundedness conditions in
D3
. Hence, the one-step estimator satisfies the expansion:

\widehat{\psi}_{K}=\psi(\widehat{h}_{K})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)+o_{p}(\sigma_{K}N^{-1/2}).

Recall that
\widehat{D}_{K}:=\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(\text{id}_{Y}-\widehat{h}_{K})
and
D_{K}:=\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta_{K})(\text{id}_{Y}-h_{K})
. By
D3
,
D2
,
D5
, and Lemma
6

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\widehat{D}_{K}(O_{ki})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}(O_{ki})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\int\widehat{D}_{K}(o_{k})P^{(K)}(do_{k})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

Thus,
\widehat{\psi}_{K}=\psi(\widehat{h}_{K})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}(O_{ki})+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\int\widehat{D}_{K}(o_{k})P^{(K)}(do_{k})+o_{p}(\sigma_{K}N^{-1/2}).

Furthermore, using the orthogonality of the projection
\left(\mathrm{I}-\Pi_{K}\right)\alpha_{K}
, the von Mises expansion in Theorem
9
, with
\bar{q}_{K}:=\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})
,
\bar{\alpha}_{K}:=\bar{\Pi}_{K}\bar{\alpha}_{K}
, and
\bar{h}_{K}:=\widehat{h}_{K}
, yields:

\displaystyle\psi(\widehat{h}_{K})

\displaystyle-\psi(h_{K})+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\int\widehat{D}_{K}(o_{k})P^{(K)}(do_{k})

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta_{K}-\widehat{\beta}_{K}),\mathcal{T}_{K}\big(\widehat{h}_{K}-h_{K}\big)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\big\langle\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K},\widehat{h}_{K}-h_{K}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle=o_{p}(\sigma_{K}N^{-1/2}),

where the final equality follows from
D5
and
D5(iii)
. Adding
\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}(O_{ki})
to both sides of the above display, we conclude that

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}_{K}-\psi(h_{K})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}(O_{ki})+o_{p}(\sigma_{K}N^{-1/2}).

Finally, by Theorem
1
and
D6
, this further implies that
\widehat{\psi}_{K}-\psi(h^{\star})=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}(O_{ki})+o_{p}(\sigma_{K}N^{-1/2})
.

Application of the Lyapunov CLT.

To apply the Lyapunov CLT, fix any
\delta>0
. By Condition
D3
,

E[|D_{K}(O_{ki})|^{2+\delta}]=O(|q(k)|^{2+\delta}),

and Condition
D4
ensures

\frac{1}{\sigma_{K}^{2+\delta}N^{1+\delta/2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}E[|D_{K}(O_{ki})|^{2+\delta}]=\frac{1}{N^{\delta/2}}\frac{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}|q(k)|^{2+\delta}}{\sigma_{K}^{2+\delta}}\to 0.

Hence, the Lyapunov condition holds, and

\frac{1}{\sigma_{K}\sqrt{N}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}(O_{ki})\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1),

so that

\frac{\widehat{\psi}_{K}-\psi(h^{\star})}{\sigma_{K}N^{-1/2}}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1).

Since
D_{K}
is bounded and has finite variance by
D3
, an application of Lindeberg’s CLT gives

\sqrt{N/\sigma_{K}^{2}}\big(\widehat{\psi}_{K}-\psi(h^{\star})\big)\overset{d}{\to}\text{N}(0,1)\text{ as }K\to\infty
,
where

\sigma_{K}^{2}:=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}q_{K}^{2}(k)\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(k)
,
with
\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(k)=\mathrm{Var}_{K}[Y_{k}-h_{K}(X_{k})]
.

∎

Proof of Theorem
7
.

Denote
\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k):=\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{j\in[n_{k}]:V_{kj}=v}\widehat{\beta}_{K}(X_{kj})
. The proof of Lemma
5
establishes that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}

\displaystyle\widehat{q}_{K,0}(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}\left\{\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\right\}\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr).

By Lemma
6
, the first term on the right-hand side of the above display satisfies the asymptotic expansion:

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}(O_{ki})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\int\widehat{D}_{K}(o_{k})P^{(K)}(do_{k})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}

\displaystyle\widehat{q}_{K,0}(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}D_{K}(O_{ki})+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\int\widehat{D}_{K}(o_{k})P^{(K)}(do_{k})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}\left\{\widehat{q}_{K,0}(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\right\}\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr).

Arguing as in the proofs of Lemma
5
and Lemma
6
, the second term satisfies:

\displaystyle\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}

\displaystyle\left\{\widehat{q}_{K,v}(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\right\}\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}\left\{\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{j\in[n_{k}]:V_{kj}=0}\beta_{K}(X_{kj})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta_{K})(k)\right\}\Bigl(Y_{ki}-h_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

Thus, arguing as in the proof of Theorem
6
for the multi-fold one-step estimator, we have that

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}_{K}^{\diamond}-\psi(h_{K})

\displaystyle=\psi(\widehat{h}_{K})-\psi(h_{K})+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}\widehat{q}_{K,0}(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}D_{K}(O_{ki})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}\left\{\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{j\in[n_{k}]:V_{kj}=0}\beta_{K}(X_{kj})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta_{K})(k)\right\}\Bigl(Y_{ki}-h_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1}\left\{\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{j\in[n_{k}]:V_{kj}=0}\beta_{K}(X_{kj})\right\}\Bigl(Y_{ki}-h_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

We conclude that

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}_{K}^{\diamond}-\psi(h_{K})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}1\{V_{ki}=1\}\left\{\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}1\{V_{ki}=0\}\beta_{K}(X_{ki})\right\}\left\{Y_{ki}-h_{K}(X_{ki})\right\}+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

Applying
D3
, noting that
o_{p}(N^{-1/2})=o_{p}(\sigma_{K}N^{-1/2})
by
D4
, and Lindeberg’s CLT gives

\sqrt{N/(2\sigma_{K}^{\diamond 2})}\,\big(\widehat{\psi}_{K}^{\diamond}-\psi(h_{K})\big)\overset{d}{\to}\text{N}(0,1)\text{ as }K\to\infty
,
where

\displaystyle\sigma_{K}^{\diamond 2}:=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}E_{K}\left[\left\{\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}1\{V_{ki}=0\}\beta_{K}(X_{ki})\right\}^{2}\left\{Y_{ki}-h_{K}(X_{ki})\right\}^{2}\right].

Some algebra then shows that

\sigma_{K}^{\diamond 2}=\sigma_{K}^{2}+\frac{2K}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{N}{Kn_{k}}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\,\sigma_{\beta}^{2}(k)\,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(k),

where
\sigma_{\beta}^{2}(k)=\mathrm{Var}_{K}[\beta_{K}(X_{k})]
,
\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(k)=\mathrm{Var}_{K}[Y_{k}-h_{K}(X_{k})]
, and
\sigma_{K}^{2}
is as defined in Theorem
6
. Notably,
\sigma_{K}^{\diamond 2}-\sigma_{K}^{2}\to 0
as
N\to\infty
provided that
K/N\to 0
,
\lim\frac{1}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{N}{Kn_{k}}<\infty
, and
\lim\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\left\{\sigma_{\beta}^{2}(k)\,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}(k)\right\}^{2}<\infty.

∎

Proof of Theorem
8
.

Observe that the one-step estimator can be written as
\widehat{\psi}_{K}^{*}=\psi(\widehat{h}_{K})+\widehat{B}_{1}+\widehat{B}_{2}
for debiasing terms
\widehat{B}_{1}
and
\widehat{B}_{2}
that we define below. By Lemma
5
with
D1
and
E4
, we have that the first debiasing term satisfies:

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{1}:=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=1-v}

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{j\in[n_{k}]:V_{kj}=v}\widehat{\beta}_{K}(X_{kj})\right\}\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(k)\Bigl(Y_{ki}-\widehat{h}_{K}\bigl(X_{ki}\bigr)\Bigr)+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

An argument identical to the proof of Lemma
5
similarly establishes that second debiasing term satisfies

\displaystyle\widehat{B}_{2}:=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{v=0,1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\mathbbm{1}\{V_{ki}=v\}\bigl(\widehat{\alpha}_{K}(X_{ki})-\widehat{\Pi}_{K}\widehat{\alpha}_{K}(X_{ki})\bigr)\bigl(\widehat{r}_{K,-v}(k)-\widehat{h}_{K}(X_{ki})\bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{N}\sum_{v=0,1}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\mathbbm{1}\{V_{ki}=v\}\bigl(\widehat{\alpha}_{K}(X_{ki})-\widehat{\Pi}_{K}\widehat{\alpha}_{K}(X_{ki})\bigr)\bigl(\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\rho}_{K})(k)-\widehat{h}_{K}(X_{ki})\bigr)+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

Denote
\widehat{\varphi}_{K}^{*}(o):=\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K})(z)\big(y-\widehat{h}_{K}(x)\big)-\big(\widehat{\alpha}_{K}(x)-\widehat{\Pi}_{K}\widehat{\alpha}_{K}(x)\big)\big(\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\rho}_{K})(z)-\widehat{h}_{K}(x)\big)
. Then, combining the previous displays, we find that

\widehat{B}_{1}+\widehat{B}_{2}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\widehat{\varphi}_{K}^{*}(O_{ki})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

Applying
D1
,
E1
-
E4
, an argument identical to the proof of Lemma
5
establishes that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\widehat{\varphi}_{K}^{*}(O_{ki})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}^{*}(O_{ki})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\int\widehat{\varphi}_{K}^{*}(o_{k})P^{(K)}(do_{k})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

By Theorem
9
and
E4
, we have

\displaystyle\psi(\widehat{h}_{K})-\psi(h_{K})+P^{(K)}\widehat{D}_{K}^{*}

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K}-\beta_{K}),\mathcal{T}_{K}\big(\widehat{h}_{K}-h_{K}\big)\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt-\big\langle\big(\text{I}-\widehat{\Pi}_{K}\big)\widehat{\alpha}_{K}-\big(\text{I}-\Pi_{K}\big)\alpha_{K}\big),\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\rho}_{K}-\rho_{K})+\widehat{h}_{K}-h_{K}\big\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle=o_{P}(N^{-1/2}).

Hence, we have the asymptotic expansion:

\displaystyle\widehat{\psi}^{*}_{K}-\psi(h_{K})

\displaystyle=\psi(\widehat{h}_{K})-\psi(h_{K})+\widehat{B}_{1}+\widehat{B}_{2}

\displaystyle=\psi(\widehat{h}_{K})-\psi(h_{K})+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\widehat{\varphi}_{K}^{*}(O_{ki})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}^{*}(O_{ki})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2})

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\psi(\widehat{h}_{K})-\psi(h_{K}))+P^{(K)}\widehat{D}_{K}^{*}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}D_{K}^{*}(O_{ki})+o_{p}(N^{-1/2}).

Arguing as in the proof of Theorem
6
and applying Lindeberg CLT, the result then follows.

∎

Appendix G
Proofs for Section
5
: Tikhonov regularization in npJIVE

G.1
Some technical lemmas

Lemma 7

(A matrix identity)
.

For any self-adjoint operator
A
, we have
(A+\lambda I)^{-1}A-I=-\lambda(A+\lambda I)^{-1}
.

Proof.

This follows from the algebra:

\displaystyle(A+\lambda I)^{-1}A-I

\displaystyle=(A+\lambda I)^{-1}(A+\lambda I-\lambda I)-I

\displaystyle=(A+\lambda I)^{-1}(A+\lambda I)-\lambda(A+\lambda I)^{-1}-I

\displaystyle=I-\lambda(A+\lambda I)^{-1}-I

\displaystyle=-\lambda(A+\lambda I)^{-1}.

∎

Lemma 8
.

Suppose that
A
and
B
satisfy, for some
0<C<\infty
, the inequality
A^{2}+\lambda B^{2}\leq C\lambda\,A^{\nu}B^{1-\nu}
. Then
A=O(\lambda^{(1+\nu)/2})
and
B=O(\lambda^{\nu/2})
.

Proof.

By Young’s inequality,
ab\leq\frac{\nu}{2}a^{2/\nu}+\frac{2-\nu}{\nu}b^{2/(2-\nu)}
. Letting
a=A^{\nu}
,
b=C\lambda B^{1-\nu}
, we obtain
C\lambda A^{\nu}B^{1-\nu}\leq\frac{\nu}{2}A^{2}+\frac{2-\nu}{\nu}(C\lambda)^{2/(2-\nu)}B^{2(1-\nu)/(2-\nu)}
. Combining with the assumption, we get
A^{2}+\lambda B^{2}\leq\frac{\nu}{2}A^{2}+\frac{2-\nu}{\nu}(C\lambda)^{2/(2-\nu)}B^{2(1-\nu)/(2-\nu)}
, hence
\frac{\nu}{2}A^{2}+\lambda B^{2}\lesssim\lambda^{2/(2-\nu)}B^{2(1-\nu)/(2-\nu)}
, which implies
B\lesssim\lambda^{\nu/2}
.

Returning to the original inequality, we have
A^{2}\leq A^{2}+\lambda B^{2}\lesssim\lambda A^{2\nu}B^{1-2\nu}\lesssim\lambda A^{2\nu}(\lambda^{\nu/2})^{1-2\nu}
, yielding
A^{2-2\nu}\lesssim\lambda^{1+\nu(1-2\nu)/2}
, so
A\lesssim\lambda^{(2+\nu-2\nu^{2})/(4(1-\nu))}\leq\lambda^{(1+\nu)/2}
, as claimed.
∎

G.2
Lemmas for primal solution

Recall that
h_{K}
is the minimum-norm solution to the inverse problem
\mathcal{T}_{K}(h_{K})=\mu_{K}
. Let
h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)
denote the unconstrained Tikhonov-regularized solution to the inverse problem, given by

h_{K}^{*}(\lambda):=\argmin_{h\in L^{2}_{K}(X)}\hskip 9.24994pt\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(h)-\mu_{K}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\lambda\|h\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

The closed-form solution is given by

h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)=\left(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mu_{K}.

For a convex function class
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{primal}}\subseteq L^{2}_{K}(X)
, let
h_{K}(\lambda)
be the constrained Tikhonov-regularized solution, given by

h_{K}(\lambda):=\argmin_{h\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{primal}}}\hskip 9.24994pt\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(h)-\mu_{K}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\lambda\|h\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

We bound the regularization bias between the unconstrained Tikhonov-regularized solution
h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)
and the minimum-norm solution
h_{K}
. This result is established in Lemma 3 of
Bennett et al. (2023c)
(see also
Carrasco et al. (2007)
), and we sketch a proof here.

Lemma 9

(Regularization bias for unconstrained solution)
.

Under
A3
, it holds that

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathcal{T}_{K}\bigl(h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)-h_{K}\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}}\,\|u_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\|h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)-h_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{\nu}\,\|u_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

Under
A2
, we have
h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)=h_{K}(\lambda)
so that the above holds for
h_{K}(\lambda)
as well.

Proof.

The unconstrained Tikhonov-regularized solution is given in closed form by

h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)=\left(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mu_{K}.

The minimum-norm solution
h_{K}
satisfies
\mathcal{T}_{K}(h_{K})=\mu_{K}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\|h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)-h_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mu_{K}-h_{K}\right\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle=\left\|\left(\left(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}-I\right)h_{K}\right\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle=\left\|-\lambda\,\left(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}h_{K}\right\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)},

where the final equality follows from Lemma
7
with
A=\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}
.

The source condition in
A3
says that
h_{K}=(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}u_{K}
for some
\nu\in[0,1]
and
u_{K}\in L^{2}_{K}(Z)
. Hence, by our previous display,

\displaystyle\|h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)-h_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle=\left\|-\lambda\,\left(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}u_{K}\right\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\left\|\left(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I\right)^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}u_{K}\right\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

Now, the spectral theorem for self-adjoint operators
(
Kato, 2013
)
shows that

\displaystyle\lambda\bigl\|(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}u_{K}\bigr\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle=\lambda\bigl\|(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}u_{K}\bigr\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{x\geq 0}\frac{\lambda\,x^{\nu}}{x+\lambda}\,\|u_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{\nu}\,\|u_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

Here, we used that for
0\leq\nu\leq 1
, the scalar function
x\mapsto\frac{\lambda\,x^{\nu}}{x+\lambda}
attains its maximum
\lambda^{\nu}
at
x=\lambda
. Hence, combining the above with our previous displays, we obtain the strong norm bound:

\|h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)-h_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\leq\lambda^{\nu}\,\|u_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

A nearly identical proof for the weak norm gives

\displaystyle\bigl\|\mathcal{T}_{K}\bigl(h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)-h_{K}\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle=\bigl\|(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigl(h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)-h_{K}\bigr)\bigr\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\,\bigl\|(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\tfrac{1}{2}}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}\,u_{K}\bigr\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda\,\sup_{x\geq 0}\frac{x^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}}}{\,x+\lambda\,}\;\|u_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}}\,\|u_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

∎

In our work, we assume that the constrained solution
h_{K}(\lambda)
equals the unconstrained one
h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)
(Conditions
A2
and
A5
), so that the above lemma can be applied to obtain fast rates. The next lemma shows that these conditions can be relaxed, potentially at the cost of a slower convergence rate. In particular, the regularization bias of the constrained solution
h_{K}(\lambda)\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{primal}}
may exhibit worse dependence on the source condition exponent
\nu
than in Lemma
9
when
\nu>1/2
. It is unclear whether this degradation is an artifact of our proof technique or a fundamental limitation. In the case where
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{primal}}
is correctly specified for
h_{K}(\lambda)
, so that
h_{K}(\lambda)=h_{K}^{*}(\lambda)
, the faster rate from Lemma
9
can still be applied.

Lemma 10

(Regularization bias with convexity constraints)
.

Assume
A3
with
\nu\in[0,1]
and
\sup_{K}\|u_{K}\|<\infty
, and that
h_{K}\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{primal}}
. If
\nu\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we have
\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(h_{K}(\lambda)-h_{K})\|=O(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\nu})
and
\|h_{K}(\lambda)-h_{K}\|=O(\lambda^{\nu})
. Otherwise if
\frac{1}{2}\leq\nu<1
, we have
\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(h_{K}(\lambda)-h_{K})\|=O(\lambda^{\frac{1+\nu}{2}})
and
\|(h_{K}(\lambda)-h_{K})\|=O(\lambda^{\frac{\nu}{2}})

Proof.

Since
h_{K}(\lambda)
minimizes the population risk over a convex function class
\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{primal}}
, the KKT conditions imply, for all
h\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{primal}}
, that

\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(h-h_{K}(\lambda)),\,\mathcal{T}_{K}(h_{K}(\lambda))-\mathcal{T}_{K}(h)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\lambda\langle h_{K}(\lambda),\,h-h_{K}(\lambda)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\geq 0.

Substituting
h=h_{K}
, we find that

\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(h_{K}-h_{K}(\lambda)),\,\mathcal{T}_{K}(h_{K}(\lambda))-\mathcal{T}_{K}(h_{K})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\lambda\langle h_{K}(\lambda),\,h_{K}-h_{K}(\lambda)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\geq 0.

Letting
e_{K}=h_{K}-h_{K}(\lambda)
, this yields the inequality

\|\mathcal{T}_{K}e_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}^{2}+\lambda\|e_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}^{2}\leq\lambda\left|\langle e_{K},h_{K}\rangle\right|.

(7)

Condition
A3
says that
h_{K}=(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}u_{K}
for some
u_{K}\in L^{2}_{K}(X)
. Hence,

\displaystyle\left|\langle e_{K},h_{K}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\langle e_{K},\,(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}u_{K}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\langle(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}e_{K},\,u_{K}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}e_{K}\|\cdot\|u_{K}\|.

For any self-adjoint operator
A
, the Heinz–Kato interpolation inequality
(
Steinerberger, 2019
)
implies

\|A^{c}x\|\leq\|Ax\|^{c}\|x\|^{1-c}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{for any }0\leq c\leq 1.

Fast rate (
0\leq\nu\leq 1/2
).
Applying the interpolation inequality with
A=(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{1/2}
, we find, for any
\nu\leq 1/2
, that

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}e_{K}\|

\displaystyle=\|A^{2\nu}e_{K}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|Ae_{K}\|^{2\nu}\|e_{K}\|^{1-2\nu}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{T}_{K}e_{K}\|^{2\nu}\|e_{K}\|^{1-2\nu}.

Combining the above bound with (
7
) and using that
\sup_{K}\|u_{K}\|<\infty
gives

\|\mathcal{T}_{K}e_{K}\|^{2}+\lambda\|e_{K}\|^{2}\lesssim\lambda\,\|\mathcal{T}_{K}e_{K}\|^{2\nu}\|e_{K}\|^{1-2\nu}.

By Lemma
8
applied with exponent
2\nu
, we have
\|\mathcal{T}_{K}e_{K}\|=O(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\nu})
and
\|e_{K}\|=O(\lambda^{\nu})
.

Slow rate (
1/2\leq\nu\leq 1
).
Applying the interpolation inequality with
A=(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})
, we find, for any
\nu\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}e_{K}\|

\displaystyle=\|A^{\nu}e_{K}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|Ae_{K}\|^{\nu}\|e_{K}\|^{1-\nu}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}(\mathcal{T}_{K}e_{K})\|^{\nu}\|e_{K}\|^{1-\nu}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\|^{\nu}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}e_{K}\|^{\nu}\|e_{K}\|^{1-\nu}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{T}_{K}e_{K}\|^{\nu}\|e_{K}\|^{1-\nu}.

Combining the above bound with (
7
) gives

\|\mathcal{T}_{K}e_{K}\|^{2}+\lambda\|e_{K}\|^{2}\lesssim\lambda\,\|\mathcal{T}_{K}e_{K}\|^{\nu}\|e_{K}\|^{1-\nu}.

By Lemma
8
applied with exponent
\nu
, we have
\|\mathcal{T}_{K}e_{K}\|=O(\lambda^{\frac{1+\nu}{2}})
and
\|e_{K}\|=O(\lambda^{\nu/2})
.

∎

G.3
Dual solution: proof of Lemma
4

Recall that
\beta_{K}(\lambda)
is the solution to the regularized inverse problem
(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)\beta_{K}(\lambda)=\alpha_{K}
, and that
\beta_{K}
is the solution to
(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})\beta_{K}=\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}

Proof of Lemma
4
.

Strong norm rate.
For the strong norm, we will establish the following two bounds:

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}-\beta_{K}\|

\displaystyle\leq O(\lambda^{\nu})\,\|w_{K}\|

(8)

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}(\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K})\|

\displaystyle\leq O(\lambda^{-1})\,\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|

(9)

Taking the above bounds as given for the moment, observe that:

\beta_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K}\;=\;\Bigl[(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\,\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}-\beta_{K}\Bigr]\;+\;(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\,(\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}).

Thus,

\|\beta_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K}\|\;=\;O(\lambda^{\nu})\,\|w_{K}\|\;+\;O(\lambda^{-1})\,\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|.

Weak norm rate.

For the weak norm, observe that:

\mathcal{T}_{K}\beta_{K}(\lambda)-\mathcal{T}_{K}\beta_{K}\;=\;\Bigl[\mathcal{T}_{K}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\,\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}-\mathcal{T}_{K}\beta_{K}\Bigr]\;+\;\mathcal{T}_{K}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\,(\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}).

We will establish the following two bounds:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}-\beta_{K}\|

\displaystyle\leq O(\lambda^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}})\,\|w_{K}\|

(10)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}(\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K})\|

\displaystyle\leq O(\lambda^{-1/2})\,\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|.

(11)

Thus,

\|\mathcal{T}_{K}\beta_{K}(\lambda)-\mathcal{T}_{K}\beta_{K}\|\;=\;O(\lambda^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}})\,\|w_{K}\|\;+\;O(\lambda^{-1/2})\,\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|.

Proof of regularization error bounds.

These bounds follow from the proof of Lemma 3 in
Bennett et al. (2023c)
. Since
\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\in\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*})
and
\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}
is invertible on
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*})
, note
\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\;=\;\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}\,\beta_{K}
implies that

(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\,\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\;-\;\beta_{K}\;=\;(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\,\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}\,\beta_{K}\;-\;\beta_{K}.

A standard argument in regularization theory shows that if

\beta_{K}=(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})^{\nu}w_{K}
, then the norm of the latter term in the previous display satisfies

\Bigl\|\Bigl[I\;-\;(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}\Bigr]\beta_{K}\Bigr\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}=O(\lambda^{\nu})\,\|w_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

For details, see the proof of Lemma
9
and the proof of Lemma 3 in
Bennett et al. (2023c)
. Hence,
\|(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\;-\;\beta_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\;=\;O(\lambda^{\nu})\,\|w_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

The proof of Lemma
9
also establishes the weak norm bound
\|\mathcal{T}_{K}((\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\;-\;\beta_{K})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\;=\;O(\lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\nu})\,\|w_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

Proof of approximation error bounds.

For the strong norm bound, consider
(A+\lambda I)^{-1}\bigl(\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\bigr),

where
A=\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}
. Then

\displaystyle\|(A+\lambda I)^{-1}(\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq\|(A+\lambda I)^{-1}\|_{\text{op}}\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\lambda}\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)},

where we use the standard bound
\|(A+\lambda I)^{-1}\|_{\text{op}}=\sup_{\sigma\in\text{spec}(A)}\frac{1}{\sigma+\lambda}\lesssim\lambda^{-1}.
For the weak norm bound, similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}(\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\|_{\text{op}}\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\sigma\in\text{spec}(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K})}\frac{\sqrt{\sigma}}{\sigma+\lambda}\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\lambda^{-1/2}\right)\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

∎

Appendix H
Proofs for Section
5
: Regret bounds in npJIVE

H.1
Local maximal inequality

Let
O_{1},\ldots,O_{N}\in\mathcal{O}
be independent random variables. For any function
f:\mathcal{O}\to\mathbb{R}
, define

\displaystyle\|f\|:=\sqrt{\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[f(O_{i})^{2}]}.

(12)

The following lemma provides a local maximal inequality and is a restatement of Lemma 9 in
Bibaut et al. (2024)
(see also Lemma 11 of
Foster and Syrgkanis (2023)
).

Lemma 11

(Local maximal inequality)
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
be a star-shaped class of functions such that
\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\|f\|_{\infty}\leq B
, and define
r_{\max}:=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\|f\|
. Let
\delta_{N}>0
satisfy the critical radius condition
\mathcal{R}_{N}(\mathcal{F},\delta_{N})\leq\delta_{N}^{2}
, where

\mathcal{R}_{N}(\mathcal{F},r):=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\|f\|\leq r}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon_{i}f(O_{i})\right],

and
\epsilon_{i}\sim\mathrm{Unif}\{\pm 1\}
are i.i.d. Rademacher random variables. Suppose that
N^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log\delta_{N}}=o(\delta_{N})
. Then, for
N
large enough so that
N^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log(1/\delta_N)}\leq\delta_{N}
, the following holds with probability at least
1-e^{-u^{2}}
for every
f\in\mathcal{F}
:

\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left(f(O_{i})-\mathbb{E}[f(O_{i})]\right)\lesssim\delta_{N}^{2}+\delta_{N}\|f\|+\frac{u\|f\|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{Bu^{2}}{N}.

Proof.

Fix any
r\geq 0
and define
\mathcal{S}(r):=\{f\in\mathcal{F}:\|f\|\leq r\}
. By Bousquet’s inequality, since each
f(O_{i})
is bounded by
B
and satisfies
\operatorname{Var}(f(O_{i}))\leq\mathbb{E}[f(O_{i})^{2}]\leq r^{2}
, we have, with probability at least
1-e^{-u^{2}}
,

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{S}(r)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(f(O_{i})-\mathbb{E}[f(O_{i})])\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{f\in\mathcal{S}(r)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon_{i}f(O_{i})\right]+\frac{ur}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{Bu^{2}}{N}.

By definition, the Rademacher complexity term is
\mathcal{R}_{N}(\mathcal{F},r):=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\|f\|\leq r}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon_{i}f(O_{i})\right]
, so the above becomes

\sup_{\|f\|\leq r}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(f(O_{i})-\mathbb{E}[f(O_{i})])\lesssim\mathcal{R}_{N}(\mathcal{F},r)+\frac{ur}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{Bu^{2}}{N}.

Since
\mathcal{F}
is star-shaped and
\mathcal{R}_{N}(\mathcal{F},\delta_{N})\leq\delta_{N}^{2}
, we have
\mathcal{R}_{N}(\mathcal{F},r)\leq(r/\delta_{N})\mathcal{R}_{N}(\mathcal{F},\delta_{N})\leq r\delta_{N}
. Substituting this yields:

\sup_{\|f\|\leq r}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(f(O_{i})-\mathbb{E}[f(O_{i})])\lesssim\delta_{N}r+\frac{ur}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{Bu^{2}}{N}.

To extend the bound uniformly over all
f\in\mathcal{F}
, apply a peeling argument. Let

r_{\max}:=\sup_{f\in\mathcal{F}}\|f\|
and

J_{N}:=\lceil\log_{2}(r_{\max}/\delta_{N})\rceil
. For
j=0,1,\dots,J_{N}
, define

r_{j}=2^{j}\delta_{N}
,

r_{-1}=0
, and

\mathcal{S}_{j}:=\{f\in\mathcal{F}:r_{j-1}<\|f\|\leq r_{j}\}
. By the fixed-
r
result applied to
r=r_{j}
, each shell satisfies, with probability at least
1-e^{-u^{2}}
,

\sup_{f\in\mathcal{S}_{j}}(P_{N}-P)f\lesssim\delta_{N}r_{j}+\frac{ur_{j}}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{Bu^{2}}{N}.

Applying a union bound over
j=0,\dots,J_{N}
, this holds for all shells simultaneously with probability at least
1-(J_{N}+1)e^{-u^{2}}
. Now fix any
f\in\mathcal{F}
and let
j
be such that
r_{j-1}<\|f\|\leq r_{j}
. Then, by monotonicity of the bound in
r
,

(P_{N}-P)f\leq\sup_{\|g\|\leq r_{j}}(P_{N}-P)g\lesssim\delta_{N}r_{j}+\frac{ur_{j}}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{Bu^{2}}{N}\lesssim\delta_{N}\|f\|+\frac{u\|f\|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{Bu^{2}}{N}.

Applying a union bound over
J_{N}=\lceil\log_{2}(r_{\max}/\delta_{N})\rceil
shells introduces an additive
\sqrt{\log J_{N}}
overhead into the deviation term. Thus, with probability at least
1-e^{-u^{2}}
, we have for every
f\in\mathcal{F}
,

(P_{N}-P)f\lesssim\delta_{N}\|f\|+\frac{(u+\sqrt{\log\log(1/\delta_N)})\|f\|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{B(u+\sqrt{\log\log(1/\delta_N)})^{2}}{N}.

Finally, using the assumption
N^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log(1/\delta_N)}=o(\delta_{N})
, we conclude that for
N
large enough so that
N^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log(1/\delta_N)}\leq\delta_{N}
, the following holds with probability at least
1-e^{-u^{2}}
for every
f\in\mathcal{F}
:

(P_{N}-P)f\lesssim\delta_{N}^{2}+\delta_{N}\|f\|+\frac{u\|f\|}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{Bu^{2}}{N}.

∎

H.2
Technical lemmas and notation

In this section, we provide technical lemmas that bound key remainder terms arising in the regret analysis of npJIVE. These bounds are derived using the local maximal inequality for empirical processes established in the previous subsection.

For a function class
\mathcal{F}
, we define the empirical Rademacher complexities:

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{N}(\mathcal{F},\delta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{v\in\{0,1\}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}:\|f\|_{L^{2}_{K}}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\frac{2}{N}\sum_{i:V_{ki}=v}\epsilon_{i}f(O_{ki}),

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n_{k}}(\mathcal{F},\delta)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}f\in\mathcal{F}:\|f\|_{k}\leq\delta\end{subarray}}\frac{2}{n_{k}}\sum_{i\in[n_{k}]:V_{ki}=0}\epsilon_{i}f(O_{ki}),

where we define the conditional norm
\|f\|_{k}:=\left\{E_{K}\left[f^{2}(O_{k})\right]\right\}^{1/2}.

Lemma 12
.

Suppose
\mathcal{F}
is a star-shaped, uniformly bounded function class. Define
\delta_{n}:=\max_{k\in[K]}\delta_{n_{k}}
and
n_{\min}:=\min_{k\in[K]}n_{k}
, where each
\delta_{n_{k}}
satisfies
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n_{k}}(\mathcal{F},\delta)]\leq\delta^{2}\sqrt{n_{k}}
. Assume
n_{k}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log(1/\delta_{n_k})}=o(\delta_{n_{k}})
. Then, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the following holds uniformly for all
f\in\mathcal{F}
and
k\in[K]
:

|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)|\lesssim\delta_{n}\|f\|_{k}+\delta_{n}^{2}+\tfrac{\log(K/\delta)}{n_{\min}}.

Proof.

Fix
\delta\in(0,1)
and set
u=\sqrt{\log(K/\delta)}
. By Lemma
11
, with probability at least
1-e^{-u^{2}}
, uniformly over all
f\in\mathcal{F}
and
k\in[K]
,

|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)|\lesssim\delta_{n_{k}}^{2}+\delta_{n_{k}}\|f\|_{k}+\frac{u\,\|f\|_{k}}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}+\frac{u^{2}}{n_{k}}.

Since
e^{-u^{2}}=\delta/K
, applying a union bound over
k
yields probability at least
1-\delta
. Moreover,

\frac{u}{\sqrt{n_{k}}}=\sqrt{\frac{\log(K/\delta)}{n_{k}}},\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{u^{2}}{n_{k}}=\frac{\log(K/\delta)}{n_{k}}.

Thus, with probability at least
1-\delta
, uniformly over all
f
and
k
,

|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)|\lesssim\delta_{n_{k}}\|f\|_{k}+\delta_{n_{k}}^{2}+\|f\|_{k}\sqrt{\frac{\log(K/\delta)}{n_{\min}}}+\frac{\log(K/\delta)}{n_{\min}}.

Finally, using
\delta_{n}=\max_{k}\delta_{n_{k}}
and absorbing the
\|f\|_{k}
-term into
\delta_{n}\|f\|_{k}
up to constants, we obtain

|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)|\lesssim\delta_{n}\|f\|_{k}+\delta_{n}^{2}+\frac{\log(K/\delta)}{n_{\min}},

as claimed.
∎

Lemma 13
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{F}
is a uniformly bounded function class. Define the function class
\mathcal{G}:=\{g_{f}:f\in\mathcal{F}\}
, where
g_{f}:=\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\{f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\}
. Let
\delta_{N}
satisfy the critical inequality
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{N}(\text{star}(\mathcal{G}),\delta)]\leq\sqrt{N}\delta^{2}
. Assume that
N^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log\delta_{N}}=o(\delta_{N})
. Then, with probability at least
1-e^{-N\delta_{N}^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(f),[\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}-\mathcal{T}_{K}](f)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\lesssim\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

Proof.

For each
v\in\{0,1\}
, we can write

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(f),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(f)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}1(V_{ki}=v)\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)\left\{f(X_{ki})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)\right\},

where the right-hand side is an empirical mean of approximately
N/2
random variables with mean zero. We will use this fact to show that this term uniformly concentrates around zero.

Define the function class
\mathcal{G}:=\{g_{f}:f\in\mathcal{F}\}
, where
g_{f}:=\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\{f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\}
. Applying the local maximal inequality stated in Lemma
11
, we find that for any
u\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-e^{-u^{2}}
, the following holds for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
:

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(f),[\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}-\mathcal{T}_{K}](f)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\lesssim\delta_{N}\|g_{f}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}+\frac{u\|g_{f}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}}{\sqrt{N}}+\frac{u^{2}}{N}.

where
\delta_{N}
satisfies the critical inequality
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{N}(\mathcal{G},\delta)]\leq\sqrt{N}\delta^{2}
. Since
\mathcal{F}
is uniformly bounded, we can further bound
\|g_{f}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}
loosely as

\displaystyle\|g_{f}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\{\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)\}^{2}E_{K}\left[\{f(X_{k})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)\}^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\left\{\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)},\,\|f\|_{L^{2}_{K}}\right\}.

Thus, for any
u\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-e^{-u^{2}}
, the following holds for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
:

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(f),[\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}-\mathcal{T}_{K}](f)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\lesssim\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\frac{u}{\sqrt{N}}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\frac{u^{2}}{N}.

Setting
u=\delta_{n}\sqrt{N}
, with probability at least
1-e^{-N\delta_{N}^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(f),[\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}-\mathcal{T}_{K}](f)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\lesssim\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

∎

Lemma 14
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{F}
be a uniformly bounded function class, and let
f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}
. Define the function class
\mathcal{G}:=\{g_{f}:f\in\mathcal{F}\}
, where
g_{f}:=f\cdot\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})
. Let
\delta_{N}
satisfy the critical inequality
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{N}(\text{star}(\mathcal{G}),\delta)]\leq\sqrt{N}\delta^{2}
. Assume that
N^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log\delta_{N}}=o(\delta_{N})
. Then, with probability at least
1-e^{-N\delta_{N}^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(f),\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}-\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(f),\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\lesssim\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

Proof.

The proof of this lemma is nearly identical to the proof of Lemma
13
. By independence of the folds, we can express
\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(f),\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}
as the empirical mean of mean zero-random variables:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}1(V_{ki}=v)\{f(X_{ki})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\}\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})(k).

Applying Lemma
11
, with probability at least
1-e^{-N\delta_{N}^{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(f-\mathcal{T}_{K}f),\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\lesssim\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

∎

Lemma 15
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{F}
is a uniformly bounded function class. Define the function class
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}:=\{\widehat{g}_{f}:f\in\mathcal{F}\}
as the set of all elements of the form
\widehat{g}_{f}:=[\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}-\mathcal{T}_{K}](f)\{f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\}
. Let
\delta_{N}
satisfy the critical inequality
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{N}(\text{star}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}}),\delta)\mid\mathcal{D}_{n,0}]\leq\sqrt{N}\delta^{2}
. Let
\delta_{n}^{2}:=\max_{k\in[K]}\delta_{n_{k}}^{2}
, where each
\delta_{n_{k}}
satisfies the critical inequality
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n_{k}}(\text{star}(\mathcal{F}),\delta)]\leq\delta^{2}\sqrt{n_{k}}
. Assume that
N^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log\delta_{N}}=o(\delta_{N})
and
n_{k}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log\delta_{n_{k}}}=o(\delta_{n_{k}})
. Then, for any
\delta>0
, with probability at least
1-\delta-e^{-N\delta_{N}^{2}}
, the following holds uniformly for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
:

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(f)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{N}\left(\delta_{n}+\sqrt{\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}}\right)\|f-f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

Lemma 16
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{F}
is a uniformly bounded function class, and let
f_{0}\in\mathcal{F}
. Define the function class
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}:=\{\widehat{g}_{f}:f\in\mathcal{F}\}
as the set of all elements of the form
\widehat{g}_{f}:=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(f)\{f-f_{0}-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\}
. Let
\delta_{N}
satisfy the critical inequality
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{N}(\text{star}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}}),\delta)\mid\mathcal{D}_{n,v}]\leq\sqrt{N}\delta^{2}
. Let
\delta_{n}^{2}:=\max_{k\in[K]}\delta_{n_{k}}^{2}
, where each
\delta_{n_{k}}
satisfies the critical inequality
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n_{k}}(\text{star}(\mathcal{F}),\delta)]\leq\delta^{2}\sqrt{n_{k}}
. Assume that
N^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log\delta_{N}}=o(\delta_{N})
and
n_{k}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log\delta_{n_{k}}}=o(\delta_{n_{k}})
. Then, for any
\delta>0
, with probability at least
1-\delta-e^{-N\delta_{N}^{2}}
, the following holds uniformly for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
:

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(f-f_{0})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{N}\left(\delta_{n}+\sqrt{\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}}\right)\|f-f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\delta_{N}\left(\delta_{n}^{2}+\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}\right)+\delta_{N}^{2}.

Proof of Lemma
15
.

We can express
\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(f)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}
as the empirical mean:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}1(V_{ki}=1)\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\right\}\left\{f(X_{ki})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)\right\}.

Moreover, from the independence of the data folds, the right-hand side is mean zero conditional on the data fold
\mathcal{D}_{n,0}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}E_{K}\Big[\big\{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)(k)

\displaystyle-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\big\}\big\{f(X_{k})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)\big\}\mid\mathcal{D}_{n,0}\Big]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\big\{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)\big\}E_{K}\big[f(X_{k})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)(k)\big]=0.

Define the function class
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}:=\{\widehat{g}_{f}:f\in\mathcal{F}\}
as the set of all elements of the form
\widehat{g}_{f}:=[\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}-\mathcal{T}_{K}](f)\{f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\}
. Note that
\big\{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\big\}\big\{f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\big\}
is an element of
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}
. Thus, applying Lemma
11
, conditionally on the training fold
\mathcal{D}_{n,0}
, we find, with probability at least
1-e^{-N\delta_{N}^{2}}
, the following holds uniformly for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
:

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(f)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\lesssim\delta_{N}\|\widehat{g}_{f}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

where
\delta_{N}
satisfies
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{N}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}},\delta)\mid\mathcal{D}_{n,0}]\leq\sqrt{N}\delta^{2}
.

Fix
\delta>0
and define
\delta_{n}^{2}:=\max_{k\in[K]}\delta_{n_{k}}^{2}
, where each
\delta_{n_{k}}
satisfies the critical inequality
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n_{k}}(\mathcal{F},\delta)]\leq\delta^{2}\sqrt{n_{k}}
. Then, by Lemma
12
, there exists a constant
C\geq 0
such that, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the following holds uniformly over all
f\in\mathcal{F}
:

\displaystyle\|\widehat{g}_{f}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|[\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}-\mathcal{T}_{K}](f)\{f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\{[\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}-\mathcal{T}_{K}](f)(k)\}^{2}\|f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\left\{\delta_{n}\|f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{k}+\delta_{n}^{2}+\frac{\log(K/\delta)}{n_{\min}}\right\}^{2}\|f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{n}^{2}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\|f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{k}^{4}\right]+\delta_{n}^{4}\|f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{L^{2}_{K}}^{2}+\frac{\log(K/\delta)}{n_{\min}}\|f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{L^{2}_{K}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left[\delta_{n}^{2}+\delta_{n}^{4}+\frac{\log(K/\delta)}{n_{\min}}\right]\|f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{L^{2}_{K}}^{2}.

Thus, we have
\|\widehat{g}_{f}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}\lesssim[\delta_{n}+\sqrt{\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}}]\|f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{L^{2}_{K}}
.

Putting it all together, we find, with probability at least
1-\delta-e^{-N\delta_{N}^{2}}
, the following holds uniformly for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
:

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(f)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{N}\left(\delta_{n}+\sqrt{\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}}\right)\|f-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f)\|_{L^{2}_{K}}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

∎

Proof of Lemma
16
.

The proof of this lemma is nearly identical to the proof of Lemma
15
. By independence of the folds, we can express
\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(f-f_{0})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}
as the empirical mean of mean zero-random variables:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\sum_{i=1}^{n_{k}}1(V_{ki}=0)f(X_{ki})\left\{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(f-f_{0})(k)-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})(k)\right\}.

Applying Lemma
11
, conditionally on the training fold
\mathcal{D}_{n,1}
, we find, with probability at least
1-e^{-N\delta_{N}^{2}}
, the following holds uniformly for any
f\in\mathcal{F}
:

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(f),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(f-f_{0})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\lesssim\delta_{N}\|f\{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(f-f_{0})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

To complete the proof, we will now show that
\|f\{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(f-f_{0})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}^{2}\lesssim\left(\delta_{n}+\sqrt{\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}}\right)\|f-f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\left(\delta_{n}^{2}+\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}\right)
with high probability. By Lemma
12
, there exists a constant
C\geq 0
such that, with probability at least
1-\delta
, the following holds uniformly over all
f\in\mathcal{F}
:

\displaystyle\|f\{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(f-f_{0})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\{[\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}-\mathcal{T}_{K}](f-f_{0})(k)\}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\left\{\left(\delta_{n}+\sqrt{\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}}\right)\|f-f_{0}-\mathcal{T}_{K}(f-f_{0})\|_{k}+\left(\delta_{n}^{2}+\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}\right)\right\}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\delta_{n}^{2}+\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}\right)\left[\sum_{k=1}^{N}\frac{n_{k}}{N}\|f-f_{0}\|_{k}^{2}\right]+\left(\delta_{n}+\sqrt{\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}}\right)^{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\delta_{n}^{2}+\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}\right)\|f-f_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}^{2}+\left(\delta_{n}+\sqrt{\log(K/\delta)/n_{\min}}\right)^{4}.

∎

H.3
Error bounds for dual solution

In the following, we define the function classes:
\mathcal{G}:=\{\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta)(\beta-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta)):\beta\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{dual}}\}\cup\{\beta\cdot\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta-\beta_{\lambda}):\beta\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{dual}}\}\cup\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{dual}}
and
\widehat{G}_{v}:=\{(\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}-\mathcal{T}_{K})(\beta)(\beta-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta)):\beta\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{dual}}\}\cup\{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(\beta)(\beta-\beta_{\lambda}-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta-\beta_{\lambda})):\beta\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{dual}}\}
.

Theorem 14

(Estimation error)
.

Assume
A5
. Let
\delta_{N}
be deterministic and almost surely satisfy the critical inequalities
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{N}(\text{star}(\mathcal{G}),\delta)]\leq\delta^{2}
and
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{N}(\text{star}(\widehat{\mathcal{G}}_{v}),\delta)\mid\mathcal{D}_{n,v}]\leq\delta^{2}
for all
v\in\{0,1\}
. Let
\delta_{n}^{2}:=\max_{k\in[K]}\delta_{n_{k}}^{2}
, where each
\delta_{n_{k}}
satisfies the critical inequality
E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{n_{k}}(\text{star}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{dual}}),\delta)]\leq\delta^{2}
. Assume that
N^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log\delta_{N}}=o(\delta_{N})
,
N\delta_{N}^{2}\rightarrow\infty
, and
n_{k}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log\delta_{n_{k}}}=o(\delta_{n_{k}})
. Then, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K}(\lambda)\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\delta_{N}\left(1+\frac{\delta_{n}+\sqrt{\log K/n_{\min}}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\lambda}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K}(\lambda)\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\lambda^{-1/2}\delta_{N}\left(1+\frac{\delta_{n}+\sqrt{\log K/n_{\min}}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\lambda}\right)\right).

Proof of Theorem
14
.

For ease of notation, we denote
R_{K}(\beta):=R_{K}^{*}(\beta)
. Denote the regularized population and empirical risks by
R_{\lambda}(\beta):=R_{K}(\beta)+\lambda\|\beta\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}
and
\widehat{R}_{\lambda}(\beta):=\widehat{R}_{K}(\beta)+\lambda\|\beta\|^{2}_{N}
, respectively. As shorthand, let
\beta_{K,\lambda}:=\beta_{K}(\lambda)
and
\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}:=\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)
denote the population and empirical risk minimizers, and denote
\mathcal{F}:=\mathcal{F}_{\mathrm{dual}}
. By
A5
, we have that
\beta_{K}(\lambda)=(\mathcal{T}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{T}_{K}+\lambda I)^{-1}\,\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}
is contained in
\mathcal{F}
.

We can lower bound the regret by:

\displaystyle R_{\lambda}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-R_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{d}{dt}R_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda}+t(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}))\big|_{t=0}+\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}R_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda}+t(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}))\big|_{t=0}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{d^{2}}{dt^{2}}R_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda}+t(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}))\big|_{t=0},

where we used the first-order optimality condition that
\frac{d}{dt}R_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda}+t(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}))\big|_{t=0}\geq 0
, since
\mathcal{F}
is convex. Computing the second derivative, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}^{2}+\lambda\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}^{2}\lesssim R_{\lambda}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-R_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda}).

(13)

Now, using that
\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}
is the minimizer of the regularized empirical risk, so that
\widehat{R}_{\lambda}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-\widehat{R}_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda})\leq 0
, we can further write:

\displaystyle R_{\lambda}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-R_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda})

\displaystyle\leq R_{\lambda}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-R_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda})-\left\{\widehat{R}_{\lambda}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-\widehat{R}_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq R_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-R_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda})-\left\{\widehat{R}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-\widehat{R}_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda})\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\lambda\left[\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}\|^{2}_{N}-\|\beta_{K,\lambda}\|^{2}_{N}-\left\{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}-\|\beta_{K,\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\right\}\right]

We now establish concentration of each of the two terms on the right-hand side of the above display.

It was shown in
Bibaut et al. (2024)
(using Lemma
11
) that, with probability tending to one:

\displaystyle\lambda\left[\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}\|^{2}_{N}-\|\beta_{K,\lambda}\|^{2}_{N}-\left\{\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}-\|\beta_{K,\lambda}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\right\}\right]\lesssim\lambda\delta_{N}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\lambda\delta_{N}^{2}.

(14)

Next, we establish concentration of
R_{\lambda}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-R_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda})-\left\{\widehat{R}_{\lambda}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-\widehat{R}_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda})\right\}
. We use the following decomposition of the empirical regret:

\displaystyle\widehat{R}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-\widehat{R}_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda})

\displaystyle=\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}-\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}-2\psi(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})

\displaystyle=\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}-2\psi(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})

\displaystyle=\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}-2\psi(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}).

Therefore, leveraging an analogous decomposition for the true regret, we have:

\displaystyle R_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-R_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda})

\displaystyle-\left\{\widehat{R}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-\widehat{R}_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}-\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\sup_{v\in\{0,1\}}\left|\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda}),\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}-\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1-v}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\right|.

We begin by establishing concentration of the second term on the right-hand side of the previous display. For each
v\in\{0,1\}
, we can write

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1-v}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle-\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda}),\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle=\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}),\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1-v}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}.

Applying Lemma
14
, we have, with probability tending to one, that

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}),\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\lesssim\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

Next, applying Lemma
16
, we have, for any
\delta>0
, with probability tending to
1-\delta
, that

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1-v}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\lesssim\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}}+\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}^{2}+\delta_{N}^{2},

where
\widetilde{\delta}_{n}:=\delta_{n}+\sqrt{n_{\min}^{-1}\log(K/\delta)}
.
Thus, we conclude, with probability tending to
1-\delta
, that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\{0,1\}}\Big|\langle

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda}),\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}-\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1-v}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}+\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}^{2}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

Next, we establish concentration of the first term on the right-hand side. We have the expansion:

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})

\displaystyle,\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}-\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle=\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}.

We consider each of the terms on the right-hand side of the above display in turn.

To bound the first term, we apply Lemma
15
to find, with probability tending to
1-\delta
, that

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}^{2}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

To bound the final two terms, we applying Lemma
13
to find for each
v\in\{0,1\}
, with probability tending to one, that

\displaystyle\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})-\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

Combining both bounds, we conclude, with probability tending to
1-\delta
, that

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,0}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})

\displaystyle,\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,1}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}-\langle\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}),\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\rangle_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle=\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}^{2}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

Substituting all our bounds, we find, with probability tending to
1-\delta
, that

\displaystyle R_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-R_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda})

\displaystyle-\left\{\widehat{R}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-\widehat{R}_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}^{2}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}^{2}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

Combining like terms, we have, with probability tending to
1-\delta
, that

\displaystyle R_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-R_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda})

\displaystyle-\left\{\widehat{R}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-\widehat{R}_{K}(\beta_{K,\lambda})\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

Therefore, from (
13
) and (
14
), we have, with probability tending to
1-\delta
, that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}^{2}+\lambda\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim R_{\lambda}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda})-R_{\lambda}(\beta_{K,\lambda})

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\lambda\left\{\delta_{N}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda\delta_{N}\left\{1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}}{\lambda}\right\}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+\delta_{N}\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}+\delta_{N}^{2}.

We now obtain weak and strong norm rates from the above inequality. Define
A:=\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}
and
B:=\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.
Then,

A^{2}+\lambda B^{2}\lesssim\lambda\delta_{N}\Bigl(1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}}{\lambda}\Bigr)B+\delta_{N}A+\delta_{N}^{2}.

Applying Young’s inequality,

\delta_{N}A\leq\frac{1}{2}A^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{N}^{2},\hskip 9.24994pt\lambda\delta_{N}\Bigl(1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}}{\lambda}\Bigr)B\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}B^{2}+\frac{\lambda\delta_{N}^{2}}{2}\Bigl(1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}}{\lambda}\Bigr)^{2}.

Substituting, we get

\displaystyle A^{2}+\lambda B^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{2}A^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}B^{2}+\frac{\lambda\delta_{N}^{2}}{2}\Bigl(1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}}{\lambda}\Bigr)^{2}+\frac{3}{2}\delta_{N}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{2}A^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}B^{2}+\delta_{N}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{(\lambda+\widetilde{\delta}_{n})^{2}}{2\lambda}+\frac{3}{2}\Bigr).

Rearranging and using that
(\lambda+\widetilde{\delta}_{n})^{2}\lesssim\lambda^{2}+\widetilde{\delta}_{n}^{2}
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}A^{2}+\frac{\lambda}{2}B^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{N}^{2}\Bigl(\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}^{2}}{2\lambda}+1\Bigr)+\lambda\delta_{N}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{N}^{2}\Bigl(1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}^{2}}{2\lambda}+\lambda\Bigr).

It follows that, with probability tending to
1-\delta
, that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\delta_{N}\left(1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\lambda}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{K,\lambda}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\lambda^{-1/2}\delta_{N}\left(1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\lambda}\right)\right).

Since
\delta>0
is arbitrary and
\widetilde{\delta}_{n}=\sqrt{\log(K/\delta)\,n_{\min}^{-1}}
, we conclude that the above bound holds unconditionally when
\widetilde{\delta}_{n}
is replaced by
\sqrt{\log K/n_{\min}}
in the
O_{p}
terms.

∎

Theorem 15

(Weak and strong norm rates)
.

Suppose that the results of Lemma
4
and Theorem
14
hold. Denote
\widetilde{\delta}_{n}:=\delta_{n}+\sqrt{\log K/n_{\min}}
. Then, for
\lambda^{\nu+1}\asymp\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}+\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\delta_{N}+\left\{\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}+\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\right\}^{\frac{2\nu+1}{2\nu+2}}\right)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\delta_{N}\left\{\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}+\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\right\}^{\frac{-1}{2\nu+2}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+O_{p}\left(\left\{\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}+\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\right\}^{\frac{2\nu}{2\nu+2}}\right).

Proof of Theorem
15
.

From Lemma
4
, we have

\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\beta_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}=O(\lambda^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}})\|w_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}+O(\lambda^{-1/2})\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

From Theorem
14
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K}(\lambda))\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle=\delta_{N}\left(1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K}(\lambda)\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{-1/2}\delta_{N}\left(1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\lambda}\right)

Combining these bounds and applying the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{-1/2}\delta_{N}\left(1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)+O(\lambda^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}})+O(\lambda^{-1/2})\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\sqrt{\lambda}\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{-1/2}\delta_{N}\left(1+\frac{\widetilde{\delta}_{n}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}\right)+O(\lambda^{\nu+\frac{1}{2}})+O(\lambda^{-1/2})\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}.

The right-hand side of the weak norm bound is minimized when
\lambda^{\nu+1}\asymp\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}+\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}
. For this choice, we find

\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K})\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}=O_{p}\left(\delta_{N}+\left\{\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}+\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\right\}^{\frac{2\nu+1}{2\nu+2}}\right).

For this choice, we find the strong norm can be bounded as

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\delta_{N}\left\{\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}+\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\right\}^{\frac{-1}{2\nu+2}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+O_{p}\left(\left\{\delta_{N}\widetilde{\delta}_{n}+\|\alpha_{K}-\Pi_{K}\alpha_{K}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}\right\}^{\frac{2\nu}{2\nu+2}}\right).

∎

Theorem 16
.

Under the conditions of Theorem
5
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K}(\lambda)\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\gamma+1}}\left(1+\frac{n^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\gamma+1}}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{-1/2}N^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\gamma+1}}\left(1+\frac{n^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\gamma+1}}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\lambda}\right).

Proof of Theorem
16
.

Note that the conditions of Lemma
4
and Theorem
14
hold under the conditions of Theorem Theorem
16
. We apply Theorem
14
using the sup-norm entropy integral to upper bound the critical radii of the function classes. To this end, we claim that the sup-norm covering numbers
N_{\infty}(\varepsilon,\mathcal{G})
and
N_{\infty}(\varepsilon,\widehat{\mathcal{G}})
are upper bounded by
N_{\infty}(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F})
, up to a constant. To see this, observe that for any
f_{1},f_{2}\in\mathcal{F}
,

\|\mathcal{T}_{K}f_{1}-\mathcal{T}_{K}f_{2}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{\infty}

and

\|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}f_{1}-\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}f_{2}\|_{\infty}\lesssim\|f_{1}-f_{2}\|_{\infty}
,
since the average of functions is upper bounded by the supremum of these functions. It follows that the sup-norm covering numbers of
\{\mathcal{T}_{K}f:f\in\mathcal{F}\}
and
\{\widehat{\mathcal{T}}_{K,v}f:f\in\mathcal{F}\}
are upper bounded by
N_{\infty}(\varepsilon,\mathcal{F})
. Furthermore, the function classes
\mathcal{G}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{G}}
are obtained through Lipschitz transformations of
\mathcal{F}
and the aforementioned function classes. Thus, by Theorem 2.10.20 of
van der Vaart and Wellner (1996)
, we conclude that

\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\delta,\mathcal{G})\lesssim\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\delta,\mathcal{F})

and

\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\delta,\widehat{\mathcal{G}})\lesssim\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\delta,\mathcal{F})
.

For any function class
\mathcal{F}
that is totally bounded in supremum norm, Dudley’s entropy integral (Lemma 4 of
van der Laan et al. (2024)
) establishes that, for any
\delta\geq N^{-1/2}
,

E_{K}[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{N}(\mathcal{F},\delta)]\lesssim N^{-1/2}\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\delta,\mathcal{F})
. Let
\delta_{N}
and
\delta_{n}
be the smallest values that satisfy the critical inequalities
N^{-1/2}\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\delta_{N},\mathcal{F})\leq\delta_{N}^{2}
and
n^{-1/2}\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\delta_{n},\mathcal{F})\leq\delta_{n}^{2}
, respectivelly. Then, by
A1
, using that
\mathcal{J}_{\infty}(\delta,\mathcal{F})\lesssim\delta^{1-1/(2\alpha)}
, we have that
\delta_{N}=O(N^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\gamma+1}})
and
\delta_{n}=O(n^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\gamma+1}})
. It can be verified that
N^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log\delta_{N}}=o(\delta_{N})
and
n_{k}^{-1/2}\sqrt{\log\log\delta_{n_{k}}}=o(\delta_{n_{k}})
. Since
\alpha>1/2
by assumption, the conditions of Theorem
14
hold. We conclude that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{T}_{K}(\widehat{\beta}_{K}(\lambda)-\beta_{K}(\lambda)\|_{L^{2}_{K}(Z)}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\gamma+1}}\left(1+\frac{n^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\gamma+1}}+\sqrt{\log K/n_{\min}}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\lambda}\right)

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\beta}_{K}-\beta_{K,\lambda}\|_{L^{2}_{K}(X)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda^{-1/2}N^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\gamma+1}}\left(1+\frac{n^{-\frac{\gamma}{2\gamma+1}}+\sqrt{\log K/n_{\min}}}{\sqrt{\lambda}}+\sqrt{\lambda}\right).

∎

H.4
Proofs for Section
5

Proof of Theorem
5
.

This is a direct application of Theorem
15
using the estimation rates of Theorem
16
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3
.

The proof of Theorem
3
is analogous to that of Theorem
5
, using regularization bias bounds in Appendix
G.2
. In particular, the estimation error bounds in Theorem
14
also hold for the primal problem. The rate without the source condition (
\nu=0
) was established in Theorem 1 of
Bibaut et al. (2024)
.
∎

Appendix I
Efficiency theory

I.1
